Kuntilanak

Malay folklore Kuntilanak with a hollow back, selected independently of neck-hole account

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Cold uncanny watchful vengeful ghost

  • Large hollow cavity structurally within upper back
Dasar sumber dan audit

Reviewed kuntilanak-c05 explicitly documents hollow-back form in Malay folklore; c04 long black hair and white dress, c06 vengeance, c07 leaning waru tree. Selected back-cavity variant without conflating neck-hole accounts.

Individually viewed generated image. Large deep oval hollow lies within pale shoulder/back edges, visibly below neck and framed by opaque white garment; hair swept aside leaves cavity clear. One side-profile head, two arms and coherent long robe covering lower body. Full silhouette inside frame; cold watchful over-shoulder stance in leaning tropical tree village scene. No blood, intimate exposure or unsupported animal traits. Feet remain covered by long robe and are not claimed individually visible.

Individually viewed generated image. Large deep oval hollow lies within pale shoulder/back edges, visibly below neck and framed by opaque white garment; hair swept aside leaves cavity clear. One side-profile head, two arms and coherent long robe covering lower body. Full silhouette inside frame; cold watchful over-shoulder stance in leaning tropical tree village scene. No blood, intimate exposure or unsupported animal traits. Feet remain covered by long robe and are not claimed individually visible.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary human woman in white dress; supernatural atmosphere only.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Pose, lighting, garment opening and composition expose documented hollow back; no new anatomical traits.
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: stylized-concept. Square premium editorial Mythics encyclopedia illustration of Kuntilanak, documented Malay hollow-back variant. One adult female ghost in modest opaque long white dress with opening revealing deep clean EMPTY hollow cavity structurally within upper back, not black fabric. Long black hair swept away from cavity, three-quarter rear view, side-profile face, one head/two arms/two legs under dress. No gore, organs, exposed intimate anatomy, invented horns/claws/fangs/wings, text/signature/watermark. Beside leaning waru in misty Indonesian village forest at night, cold watchful vengeful aura. Mature detailed painterly book illustration, antique-gold moonlight, walnut/forest-green environment. Full figure inside square frame.

Banshee

Banshee's documented whole-crow manifestation discussed by Emily Zarka in PBS Monstrum

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Mournful sorrowful watchful familial death-messenger rather than attacker

  • Entire avian crow/corvid body with feathered wings, bill and clawed feet
Dasar sumber dan audit

Emily Zarka's original PBS Monstrum educational program Banshee: Ireland's Screaming Harbinger of Death (25 Sept 2019), transcript directly states some banshees have crow shapeshifting ability in its Irish tradition discussion. Uses that rare animal variant with current creature's herald-of-death research, not making every banshee avian or identifying the spirit as Badb herself. Donald Mackenzie separately records raven/hoodie-crow in Scottish named Banshee traditions; this corroboration is regional and not conflated with universal Irish identity.

Individually inspected via view_image. One wholly black corvid bird head/bill, two natural wings folded against one feathered body, two legs with separately visible feet gripping stone wall, full feathered tail inside frame. No human features, extra limbs, horns, costume or hybrid. Open bill, alert sad gaze, quiet dark lake and candlelit old Irish house preserve mournful familial death-messenger aura rather than predator action. No text, watermark or border. Exact crow/raven species deliberately not claimed; both named corvid variants are documented regionally.

Individually viewed original. One whole black corvid with two natural feet, folded wings, open beak and feathered tail. Somber calling pose by Irish rural home matches explicitly selected crow death-messenger manifestation, no invented features.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary human woman in green dress, no visibly altered anatomy.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Natural black corvid rendering without exact taxonomic species claim; feather/bill details interpreted
  • Irish stone-wall family-house setting conveys messenger association; no invented anatomy
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: scientific-educational. ONE square premium painterly folklore encyclopedia illustration of BANSHEE in the documented WHOLE CROW animal manifestation discussed by Emily Zarka in PBS Monstrum, rather than the customary woman portrait. ONE entirely black crow perched on an old Irish stone wall beside an old family house and quiet dark water at dusk. One natural crow head with strong black bill, one coherent feathered avian body, exactly TWO wings slightly separated from body and exactly TWO dark bird legs/feet gripping wall, one normal feathered tail; entire head, wing tips, feet and tail inside frame. NO human face, hands, torso, costume, horns or hybrid anatomy. Crow's bill slightly open as though uttering a mournful keen, alert grief-stricken watchfulness, quiet sorrowful death-messenger aura rather than malicious killer or attacking demon. Background old Irish stone cottage window with faint distant warm candle glow, evening mist over dark river and deep forest green countryside, walnut weathered masonry, subtle antique gold rim lighting. Refined naturalistic premium oil-painted texture and detailed iridescent black feathers. Bird alone; no other animals, people, bodies or victims. No magical jewels, crown, weapons, gore, text, inscription, signature, frame or watermark. This is one source-attested animal manifestation of the banshee, whose role is to mourn and foretell death, not cause it. Preserve that sorrowful herald aura.

Baba Yaga

Baba Yaga with an extraordinarily long literal iron nose and sharp iron teeth, recorded by Ralston

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Formidable knowing forest tester, threatening and ambiguous

  • Exceptionally elongated literal metal nose
  • Sharp metal tooth row
Dasar sumber dan audit

Ralston's historical folklore accounts expressly provide iron nasal and dental anatomy. The familiar bony-leg epithet was not interpreted as an exposed skeleton.

Individually inspected via view_image. One gaunt hag with one head, two arms/hands and two feet inside frame. Very long dark metallic nose protrudes directly from facial bridge without mask straps, sharply reflecting light; open grin contains dark-metal pointed teeth. No horns, wings, tail, claw hands or skeletal wounds. Broom, mortar and pestle present; knowing intimidating expression preserves her ambiguous forest aura. No visible text/signature.

Independently viewed: gaunt old hag with long metal nose, sharp metal teeth, two hands and feet, pestle, broom and mortar plus chicken-legged hut; no image text. Ralston variant supported; setting combines familiar attributes.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary elderly woman with mortar and broom; props do not change anatomy.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact nose length/curve, hammered metal texture, seated porch pose, tool arrangement and dress/lighting are aesthetic interpretation of the recorded iron-nose form. A small chicken-legged hut in the distance is setting, not a replacement for Baba Yaga.
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: illustration-story. Square premium mature painterly Slavic folklore encyclopedia illustration of ONE BABA YAGA selecting Ralston's source-attested LONG IRON NOSE manifestation (Russian Fairy Tales 1873 p147; Songs of Russian People 1872 p161 also records iron nose and strong sharp iron teeth). She is a very tall gaunt elderly supernatural hag with dishevelled grey hair sitting sideways on a low rough stool in the shadowed porch of her forest hut. Entire lean figure visible: one head, two arms/hands and two bony legs/feet, within frame. Essential nonhuman anatomy: an extraordinarily LONG LITERALLY IRON NOSE growing seamlessly from her face, dark hammered iron with an unmistakable metallic surface, extending out and downward almost the length of her forearm; a slightly open grin reveals a row of sharp IRON TEETH. These metal parts ARE HER ANATOMY, not a mask, prosthetic, beak, costume or object held before her face. Her eyes are alert and knowing, expression ambiguous threatening trickster and formidable tester. Faded modest earth-coloured dress, no revealing clothing, no exposed skeletal wounds. One hand rests on a wooden pestle, the other holds a plain broom; a wooden mortar rests next to her as source-compatible flight tools. Forest hut doorway and pine/birch roots visible; background chicken-legged hut may be hinted but the main focal subject is Baba Yaga herself, not house. Deep forest green shadows and antique-gold fire/moonlight, textured sophisticated brushwork. Make extraordinarily long metallic nose unmistakable, not a normal human nose. No horns, wings, animal ears, invented claws, monster tail, reptile face, extra limbs, little girl, other people, severed parts, skull fence, gore, blood, text, border, artist signature or watermark.

Wewe Gombel

Javanese Wewe Gombel with documented elongated breasts and claws, modest opaque treatment

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Stern protective yet uncanny elderly forest spirit

  • Long sharp recurved finger claws visible on both hands
  • Two distinctly elongated pendulous breasts covered by opaque cloth
Dasar sumber dan audit

Reviewed c05 records exceptionally large breasts and c13 explicitly documents elongated breasts and claws. c06 supports sheltering neglected children without harming them, informing non-attacking protective aura.

Individually inspected full generated image. Elderly gaunt woman has two arms/hands with visibly long pointed recurved claws. Two separate elongated upper-chest forms hang beneath opaque dark cloth to waist/hips; narrow flat abdomen visible between, no rounded pregnant belly. Entire body covered, no nudity. One face, two arms and covered lower body fully framed. Banyan/Central Javanese hillside and stern watchful mood retained. No horns, wings, extra limbs, children, blood or text.

Individually viewed original. Two very long separately cloth-covered chest forms and two clawed hands, slim abdomen, one whole elderly spirit. Claws visibly curve well beyond normal nails. Entire opaque dress covers intimate anatomy; stern guardian/haunter aura and banyan setting.

Alasan penolakan lama: Human elderly woman in black clothing without clearly distinct anatomy.

Božalość

Sorbian white-clad death messenger

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One little white-clad female death messenger with long unkempt hair and red eyes, weeping under a dark cottage window beside an elder bush in Lusatia. Human anatomy retained, eyes bloodshot/red rather than lasers.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; red eyes, weeping death-omen woman.
Dasar sumber dan audit

Božalość, also transliterated Božaloshtsh or Bozaloshtsh, is a messenger of misfortune or death in Wendish mythology (the Wends, Lusatian Sorbs). An 1886 article, Das Spreewaldhaus by W. v. Schulenburg, links it with the elder bush and describes it as a woman in white with long, unkempt hair and red eyes. Biren Bonnerjea describes it as a little long-haired woman who weeps under the window of someone about to die.

Individually viewed: solitary white-clad human-shaped woman under cottage window with elder flowers, long unkempt dark hair, visible bloodshot red eyes and tears. Both hands coherent, no invented features. Dress extends to lower edge so feet not exposed; documented identifying face/clothing and weeping pose visible. Moonlit ominous atmosphere, no text or watermark.

Independently viewed original: White-clad long-haired weeping female apparition with reddened eyes and tears below cottage window, elder blossoms; coherent human anatomy. No visible text or watermark. Source-shaped human figures accepted under clarified user policy.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary woman in white dress.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact facial likeness, clothing folds, compositional details and painterly lighting are illustrative; no anatomical deformation asserted beyond selected documented account.
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: illustration-story. Asset type: ONE square premium Mythics mythology encyclopedia artwork. Subject: Božalość, Sorbian messenger of death, selecting Schulenburg's 1886 white-clad long-haired red-eyed woman, also Bonnerjea's small woman weeping under a window. One small adult woman with normal human anatomy in a modest plain white long dress, long unkempt dark hair, clearly visible tear-reddened RED EYES and mournful expression, quietly weeping under the dark window of a rural Lusatian cottage beside an elder bush. Do not turn metaphor into anatomy: red eyes may be bloodshot; no supernatural deformation is claimed. Full coherent figure, two normal arms and hands and two legs beneath cloth, comfortably inside frame. The ominous death-omen presence is conveyed by loneliness, moonlit elder leaves, cold mist and a dark silent window, not invented horns or fangs. Detailed refined painterly folklore illustration, earthy dark forest-green shadows, moonlight whites and restrained warm copper window frame; solemn uncanny supernatural mood. No companions, skull props, glowing magic symbols, wings, horns, animal limbs, exaggerated teeth, gore, sensuality, readable text, border, signature or watermark.

Fish-man

Liérganes Fish-Man in his early sea-return folkloric form with front scale strip and apparent neck gills

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Withdrawn distant seaborne presence of the returned amphibious figure

  • Overlapping scale strip anatomically continuous along front torso
  • Recessed apparent gill slits on both neck sides
Dasar sumber dan audit

Verified research fish-man-c06 supplies scale strip and apparent neck gills; Feijoo §II.15 independently records early scales and explains their later disappearance. This depicts the legendary early form, not historical biological truth.

Individually inspected via view_image. One coherent adult figure with one head, two arms/hands and two legs/feet fully inside frame. Clearly overlapping fish scales run from front neck to lower belly; both neck sides show repeated recessed gill-like slits. These structural amphibious traits are clearly visible instead of mere pale skin/costume. Pale skin and short red hair match c06; rough cloth covers lower body. No fins, tail, fish head, wings or extra limbs. No visible signature/text.

Independently viewed: red-haired human-shaped figure with clearly visible frontal fish-scale strip and bilateral neck gill slits, no tail or added fins; coherent anatomy, coastal setting.

Alasan penolakan lama: Human man with subtle stripe of scales; overall ordinary human portrait.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact scale colour, strip width, gill slit count/depth, cloth and coastal stance/lighting are visual interpretations of the record. No additional piscine appendages.
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: illustration-story. Square premium mature painterly Cantabrian folklore encyclopedia artwork of the FISH-MAN OF LIÉRGANES / Francisco de la Vega Casar at his legendary early return from the sea. Follow the documented description: one pale white-skinned young adult male with SHORT THIN RED HAIR, human-shaped head and body, a broad conspicuous vertical STRIP OF OVERLAPPING FISH SCALES running continuously from the front of his throat down the centre of his chest to his belly, and apparent GILL OPENINGS on both sides of his neck. These are anatomical fish scales and actual recessed gill-like slits, never tattoos, painted skin or clothing. The scale strip and neck gills must be unmistakably visible and make the legendary amphibious anatomy clear, not an ordinary man. Three-quarter view standing on a wet rocky coast by the Bay of Cádiz at dusk, chest toward viewer with neck turned slightly so gill openings visible. A modest rough cloth covers waist, hips and thighs; no genitals or eroticism. Exactly one head, two natural arms and hands, two legs and feet, all hands and feet inside frame. Quiet distant withdrawn seaborne aura rather than hostile combat. Wet skin/scale texture, dark coastal sea, antique-gold horizon and deep forest-green shadows, sophisticated painterly brushwork. No fish tail, fins, webbed hands, horns, wings, fish head, additional limbs, invented hybrid anatomy, other people, nets constricting body, gore, text, border, signature or watermark. This is a folkloric interpretation of recorded scales and apparent gills; not a biological fact claim about a historical person.

Male hairy hideous Caucasian Almaz with axe embedded in chest

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Sinister territorial feral forest spirit

  • Entire densely shaggy hair-covered bodily surface
  • One axe embedded non-graphically in the chest rather than carried in the hands
Dasar sumber dan audit

Reviewed almaz-mythical-beast-c02 explicitly records hairy hideous males with an axe embedded in the chest, selected instead of female golden-haired humanlike variant c03.

Individually inspected generated image: one head, two arms/hands and two legs/feet, all body densely shaggy from skin rather than fur clothing; rough hideous wild face. Single axe blade rests partly into upper chest hair/body and handle protrudes, neither hand holds it. No strap or blood. Modest opaque waistcloth, full feet and axe fit frame. No invented horns, wings or animal muzzle. Caucasus forest/rock setting and menacing watchful aura retain documented male beast form.

Individually viewed original. Full shaggy beast body with bodily fur on torso/arms/legs, one axe embedded non-graphically in chest, hands empty. No invented horns/wings; stern mountain wild-being morphology matches documented male variant.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary blonde woman in dress, no visible nonhuman anatomy.

Aloja (mythology)

Water-blackbird (dipper) animal transformation of Catalan aloja

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Quiet kind mysterious freshwater fairy in animal guise

  • Wholly avian body with wings, beak, bird feet and feathered tail
Dasar sumber dan audit

Research explicitly describes aloja turning into water blackbird/dipper and dwelling near Pyrenean freshwater.

Individually viewed complete image: single fully avian white-throated brown dipper with one natural beak/head, coherent feathered torso, folded wing plumage, two clearly visible scaly bird feet and one modest tapered tail. No woman or hybrid anatomy. Wet rock and cold Pyrenean stream under moonlight preserve quiet benign nocturnal freshwater-spirit aura. No added horns, humanoid features, jewellery, text or watermark. Represents explicit source-attested bird transformation.

Viewed full original independently: one complete dipper bird with coherent single avian head/beak, feathered wings, two visible feet and tail, no human anatomy. Calm moonlit freshwater scene preserves benevolent aura. Latest researched claim aloja-mythology-c01 explicitly supports water-blackbird animal transformation, and c08 supplies the freshwater habitat; natural species plumage is disclosed interpretation.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary woman at waterside, no visible nonhuman anatomy.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Natural white-throated brown dipper plumage rendered as species interpretation
  • Moonlit river setting and painterly lighting
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Square premium detailed painterly Mythics encyclopedia illustration of ALOJA, Catalan water fairy in source-attested DIPPER / WATER BLACKBIRD animal-transformation guise. ONE recognizably natural white-throated brown-grey dipper bird stands on one wet river rock in a cold blue Pyrenean mountain stream at full-moon twilight. Full bird in three-quarter side view, exactly one small bird head, one pointed dark beak, two folded feathered wings, two natural bird legs/feet, one short feathered tail. All body and both feet visible uncropped. No woman, human face, human torso, magical fairy wings or mixed humanoid hybrid. Gentle alert contemplative bird posture, quiet benevolent freshwater guardian atmosphere, mossy stream stones and distant hazy Pyrenean slopes. Refined painterly realism, forest green and walnut surroundings, small gold glints on water and feathers against cool nocturnal blues, rich subtle feather texture. One bird only, no horns, crown, jewellery, extra limbs or supernatural appendages, no text, symbols, frame or watermark. Contemporary artistic interpretation of the documented dipper transformation, no claim that every aloja has bird anatomy.

Busaw

Busaw in its corpse-stealing night form with pointed teeth, hooked nails and long tongue

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Hungry sneaking corpse-thief menace beneath a large tropical tree

  • Very long protruding tongue
  • Sharply pointed tooth rows
  • Long strongly hooked nails on coherent hands
Dasar sumber dan audit

Verified claim busaw-c02 records human-seeming day appearance but pointed teeth, hooked nails and long tongue for the corpse-thieving ghoul; it resides in large trees near cemeteries and substitutes banana trunks for corpses.

Individually inspected via view_image. One coherent crouching ghoul with one head, two arms, two hands and two legs/feet within frame. Mouth contains clearly pointed teeth and one long tongue hanging well below jaw toward chest; hands have sharply curved hooked fingernails. These structural features make the figure visibly nonhuman, rather than an ordinary man. No horns, wings, tail or long elf ears. Tropical tree, cemetery and a green cut plant trunk support the corpse-thief scene, without a visible corpse or gore.

Individually viewed original. One long tongue extending to chest, pointed teeth and two long recurved claw hands. Coherent two-arm/two-leg crouching body fully in frame. No extra limbs/wings/horns; predatory night graveyard aura matches documented corpse-eater.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary woman in woodland clothing, no nonhuman physical characteristics visible.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact lengths of tongue and hooked nails are painterly interpretations of the attested long/hooked descriptions, rather than measured folkloric dimensions. Gaunt crouch, hair, plain cloth, lighting and cemetery details are aesthetic choices.
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: illustration-story. Square premium mature painterly folklore encyclopedia artwork of one BUSAW, Philippine corpse-stealing tree ghoul, displaying ONLY the source-attested night anatomy: an open jaw full of sharply POINTED TEETH, one conspicuously LONG TONGUE extending down toward its chest, and pronounced HOOKED NAILS visibly curving from each finger. This must unmistakably read as the documented nonhuman ghoul, never an ordinary person in makeup or costume. One hunched gaunt human-shaped body in a crouch at the base of a large tropical tree beside a distant quiet cemetery. A single broad grimacing face showing pointed teeth and the one long tongue, natural humanlike ears. Exactly two arms, two hands, two legs, two feet, one head, one tongue; fingers form coherent hands and end in hooked nails, not additional limbs. All hands and feet visible within frame. Its worn neutral cloth is plain and secondary, not modern horror clothing. No animal skull, horns, wings, tail, long elf ears, additional mouths, invented hybrid body parts. Humid tropical foliage, roots, subdued grave markers and one cut banana trunk lying beside roots refer to its corpse-swapping folklore; no victim or actual corpse. Antique-gold low moonlight, deep forest greens, dark earth, rich tactile brushwork, hungry sneaking graveyard menace. Non-graphic: no gore, blood, severed body, viscera, wounds. No text, border, watermark. Structural pointed teeth, single long tongue and hooked fingernails must be clearly readable in the silhouette and face.

Ghillie Dhu

Contemporary Scottish educational retelling with seasonal green skin

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: Shy gentle protective woodland fairy

  • Green seasonal skin throughout face and bare limbs distinct from leafy costume
Dasar sumber dan audit

Reviewed original folklore supplies solitary dark-haired leafy-clothed Gairloch birch fairy. School-hosted later educational retelling adds seasonal green skin; image explicitly selects that contemporary retelling and does not backdate skin detail.

Individually inspected: clearly green skin face, near arm, near hand, both lowerlegs and both bare feet under warm gold natural lighting; green is physiology not overall scene tint or leafy costume. Black hair and brown eyes, leaves/moss tunic and shorts, birch habitat/lake and berries nuts retained. One head/visible arm/hand; far arm naturally hidden behind birch, not extra or severed. Whole body incl feet fits square. No pointed-ear additions, horns/wings/tail, violence or text.

Independently viewed: seasonal green skin including bare arms and feet, black hair, leafy/moss clothes in birch woods; later educational retelling explicitly selected rather than attributed to earliest account.

Alasan penolakan lama: Ordinary bearded man wearing foliage, human anatomy and costume.

Leprechaun

Traditional red-clad solitary shoemaker fairy

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One diminutive bearded old male Irish solitary fairy wearing historical RED coat and red cap rather than modern green outfit, quietly cobbling a small shoe with an awl on a low woodland root bench. Normal rounded human ears and human limbs, old wrinkled mischievous face. Giant clover leaves and tree roots establish tiny scale, a humble buried crock with a few gold coins half visible beside him supports treasure tradition. Calm uncanny secret craft at twilight, restrained magical aura from scale and mood only.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; miniature bearded solitary shoemaking fairy in historical red dress.
Dasar sumber dan audit

A leprechaun (Irish: leipreachán) is a diminutive supernatural being of Irish folklore, a solitary fairy, a little bearded man of mischief, and a shoemaker with a pot of gold at the rainbow's end. The leprechaun is a solitary creature whose main work is making and cobbling shoes and who enjoys practical jokes. According to William Butler Yeats, leprechauns' wealth comes from treasure-crocks buried in war-time. Before the 20th century the leprechaun was generally held to wear red, not green.

Individually viewed: tiny elderly bearded shoemaker in red coat and cap, coherent human hands working awl on shoe, root bench and huge clover establish scale, nearby treasure crock. Human ear outer curve and aged face visible, no animal or wing anatomy. Full shoes visible, no text or watermark.

Independently viewed original: Tiny bearded shoe-cobbler with red coat/cap, awl and shoe; coherent hands/feet, clover scale and nearby gold. Visible ear appears natural aged auricle, not exaggerated fantasy ear. No visible text or watermark. Source-shaped human figures accepted under clarified user policy.

Alasan penolakan lama: Short elderly human man in hat and red coat, scale and costume only.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact facial likeness, clothing folds, compositional details and painterly lighting are illustrative; no anatomical deformation asserted beyond selected documented account.

Moss people

Grey hairy moss-clad elderly forest-folk variant

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One child-sized but elderly grey hairy human-shaped forest spirit of German Moss People, modestly clothed in actual moss-clad woven garments, standing beside a hollow old forest tree and holding a few healing herbs. Short old figure with grey hairy face, ordinary rounded human ears, both coherent hands visible, human limbs. Moss is clothing, NEVER botanical tissue or tree skin. Forest roots and hollow trunk establish size, protective herbal healing forest atmosphere, no invented tree-face or branch hands.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; child-sized grey hairy old forest spirit wearing moss garments, associated with trees and healing herbs.
Dasar sumber dan audit

Moss people (Moosleute), also wood folk (Holzleute) or forest folk (Waldleute), are a class of fairy-folk compared with dwarfs, elves or spirits, described in German folklore as intimately tied to trees and the forest. Moss people are sometimes like dwarfs, child-sized but grey, old-looking, hairy and moss-clad; sometimes bigger, and in other accounts pretty. Moss people, especially females, can send plagues yet also heal their victims; in epidemics the Holzfräulein came out of the forest to show which herbs cure or prevent plague. Moosweiblein live in the forest, on heaths, in dark places or the deepest woods; they dwell in earth holes, hollow trees, root huts or moss huts, sleep on moss and bed their children on moss or bark.

Individually viewed: small grey-haired/bearded aged human-shaped forest spirit with coherent hands and herbs, moss visibly layered over woven clothing and boots beside hollow tree. Skin remains human, no botanical body/branch hands or invented pointed ears. Entire figure inside frame, no text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Small old grey-haired forest spirit in moss-covered clothes, holding herbs beside hollow tree; clothes are distinct from coherent human skin/body. No visible text or watermark. Source-shaped human figures accepted under clarified user policy.

Alasan penolakan lama: Small ordinary human bearded man wearing moss; costume and scale do not change anatomy.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact facial likeness, clothing folds, compositional details and painterly lighting are illustrative; no anatomical deformation asserted beyond selected documented account.
Receipt lengkap dan sumber
Use case: illustration-story. Asset type: ONE individual square premium painterly mythology encyclopedia artwork for Mythics. Subject and selected documented variant: One child-sized but elderly grey hairy human-shaped forest spirit of German Moss People, modestly clothed in actual moss-clad woven garments, standing beside a hollow old forest tree and holding a few healing herbs. Short old figure with grey hairy face, ordinary rounded human ears, both coherent hands visible, human limbs. Moss is clothing, NEVER botanical tissue or tree skin. Forest roots and hollow trunk establish size, protective herbal healing forest atmosphere, no invented tree-face or branch hands. Style: refined detailed painterly illustrated folklore book, believable materials, readable anatomy, restrained copper/antique-gold and deep forest-green atmosphere while preserving documented colors. Composition: solitary central subject, essential identifying features unobscured and comfortably within edges. Preserve SOURCE-SUPPORTED human morphology whenever this is the documented form; do not invent monstrosity. Supernatural character through the described role, environment and atmosphere. No text, captions, border, logo, signature, watermark, gore or sexualization. Exactly one coherent subject unless an explicitly requested mount. No extra heads or limbs.

Dasar sumber dan audit

The Buckriders (Dutch Bokkenrijders, French Chevaliers du bouc) belong to south-eastern Dutch and north-eastern Belgian folklore: witches flying on bucks from the Devil to rob and murder people and church property, who once a year visited the Devil on the Mook Heath. It was believed the buckriders could travel fast and far through the skies to rob over a wide area and be home before dawn, hiding their crimes.

Individually viewed: one human witch riding a coherent horned male goat in night sky over rural church/countryside. Goat four legs and two horns visible, no wings; rider normal limbs with both hands on reins. Full goat/rider framed, no victims or text/watermark.

Fenixmännlein

Silesian mountain-dwelling earth sprite baker

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One small human-shaped Silesian earth sprite, intermediate in size between a dwarf and a human, in modest worn old rural clothing with ordinary rounded ears, taking a cabbage-like baked cake from a stone oven in a hill cave. The warmly lit cavern is cozy yet mysteriously hidden, smoke rises toward a small earth opening overhead. One figure, no invisible duplicate hand. Supernatural secret baker atmosphere; no flames shaped into phoenix wings or invented bird anatomy.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; earth sprite in mountain cave associated with hidden baking and cave smoke.
Dasar sumber dan audit

The Fenixmännlein (also Fenixmännel, Fenskemännel, Fenesleute) are earth sprites and dwarfs of German folklore, once known in Silesia, especially the Oderské vrchy and Hrubý Jeseník, and in the Saxon part of Lusatia. Derivation from the Venedigermännlein ('little Venetian men') is supported since both are bigger than dwarfs but smaller than humans; the Fenixmännlein live collectively in hills and shrubbery. Inside the mountains they hold big parties; they are said to own the original Silesian streuselkuchen recipe, and when they bake in their caves smoke rises from earth holes. A ploughboy who asked for cake was at once handed a cabbage cake with linseed oil by an invisible hand, threatened to eat it on an iron table; he used his ploughshare as that table.

Individually viewed: one small male human-shaped earth sprite removing cabbage-like cake from cave stone oven with both coherent hands on wooden peel. Human rounded ear, beard, full boots, smoke toward earth opening overhead, no phoenix features or text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Small human-shaped earth spirit using two-handed peel to bake cabbage-like cake in cave oven, smoke toward earth opening; no phoenix anatomy. No visible image text or watermark.

Alasan penolakan lama: Short but ordinary human baker holding cake; no source-supported nonhuman anatomy.

Pilihan artistik:

  • Exact facial likeness, clothing folds, compositional details and painterly lighting are illustrative; no anatomical deformation asserted beyond selected documented account.

Türst

Rickenbach green-clad ghostly huntsman

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One dreadful but human-shaped Swiss spectral huntsman Türst, selecting Rickenbach GREEN hunting dress. Adult stern man in green coat and old hat sounding a curved hunting horn with one normal hand; other hand relaxed.   • No anatomical deformation claimed; green-clad dreadful huntsman blowing horn in storm, a supernatural hunt leader.
Dasar sumber dan audit

Türst is a folk figure of Lucerne's farming communities dating to pre-Christian times, a 'dreadful huntsman' to beware of in stormy weather, who blows his horn through villages in the stormy months before Epiphany with a pack of three-legged hounds. In Rickenbach he appears in green hunting dress with a half-dog, half-pig pack; in Wolhusen people raised crosses against him, and a fallen cross brought farmers bad luck until set upright; the people of Pilatus call him a ghost and a 'dreadful huntsman'. Türst (also called Dürst) is a legendary figure from the Lucerne hinterland dating from heathen times, called the 'hellish huntsman'. Barn doors should be left open at this time so Türst can hunt through unhindered; his wife Sträggele appears in some legends, and others say he terrifies dairy cows until they sicken and stop giving milk.

Individually viewed: solitary normal human-shaped huntsman in green dress sounding curved hunting horn, coherent two hands/legs, stormy snowy Lucerne farm landscape and open barn. A minor cow head is visible deep in barn as background setting consistent with recorded dairy context, not an invented companion or anatomy; no dog morphology applied to huntsman. No text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Human ghostly huntsman in green clothing blowing horn beside open snowy barn; coherent limbs. Small background cow contextual, not creature anatomy. No visible image text or watermark.

Alasan penolakan lama: Main subject is human huntsman in hunting clothes; hounds do not make his anatomy nonhuman.

Mótsognir

Motsognir after divine gift of human shape

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One human-shaped principal Norse dwarf Mótsognir, selecting Gylfaginning's stage AFTER gods bestowed HUMAN SHAPE and understanding. A short ancient dignified adult male with natural human face, rounded ears, heavy beard and modest plain archaic wool clothing seated alone in a primordial earth and rock cavern, one ordinary hand resting on stone and the other open in a quiet thoughtful gesture.   • No anatomical deformation claimed; principal dwarf in documented human-shaped post-origin stage.
Dasar sumber dan audit

Mótsognir ('he who drinks in might'), also Móðsognir in the Hauksbók variant ('he who drinks in courage'), is ruler of the dwarfs; Völuspá 10 calls him 'lord of all the dwarves'. Per Snorri in Gylfaginning 14, the dwarfs first took shape and life in Ymir's flesh as maggots, then by the gods' decision gained awareness, intelligence and human shape though living in earth and rocks; Modsognir was a dwarf and the second was Durinn. According to Völuspá 9, the dwarfs were made by 'the high holy gods', who resolved to shape them 'out of Brimir's blood and Blainn's bones', usually read as the blood and flesh of the primordial giant Ymir. Modsognir or Motsognir is the first dwarf in Norse mythology, created by the gods; the meaning of the name is disputed, most likely 'the weary, powerless one'.

Individually viewed: one principal human-shaped dwarf in primordial mineral cavern, normal human face/rounded ear, coherent resting and open hands, two visible boots. Source-supported post-divine-human-shape stage, no larva or personally undocumented animal form, no crown or smith attribute. No text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Principal dwarf in documented human-shaped post-origin stage, coherent hands/ear/face and two boots in cavern. Supersedes unsupported personal larval portrayal; no invented anatomy. No visible image text or watermark.

Erlking

Goethe literary bearded crowned forest spirit

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One sinister human-shaped elderly forest elf-king from Goethe's literary Erlkönig, long beard and simple crown atop white hair, wearing a modest long archaic cloak, reaching one ordinary hand from among dark alder trunks. Full solitary figure contained within picture, no child or traveller visible. Silent deeply unsettling predatory woodland mood from night, mist and facial expression. Ordinary rounded human ears, no tree-bark body, antlers, wings or animal anatomy. A literary supernatural king portrait rather than a literal alder-tree hybrid.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; sinister supernatural king among alder trees.
Dasar sumber dan audit

In European folklore and myth the Erlking is a sinister elf lingering in the woods, stalking children who stay too long and killing them with a single touch. The name Erlking (Erlkönig, 'alder-king') was used in German Romanticism for a spirit or 'king of the fairies' and is usually thought to derive from Danish folklore's ellekonge (older elverkonge, 'elf-king'). The name was first used by Johann Gottfried Herder in Erlkönigs Tochter (1778), adapted from the Danish ballad Hr. Oluf han rider (1739), then taken up by Goethe in Erlkönig (1782), set by Schubert and others; Goethe added a new sense, 'Erl' meaning 'black alder', not 'elf'.

Individually viewed: sinister crowned long-bearded human-shaped forest king among misty alder trunks, coherent normal extended hand and relaxed opposite hand. No tree body, antlers, wings, child or visible attack. Crown supported by Goethe primary poem, beard/aged appearance are portrait illustration choices. No text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Crowned sinister human-shaped forest spirit with coherent reaching hand, no literal tree body or child victim; Goethe literary variant. No visible image text or watermark.

Alasan penolakan lama: Elf king character with human anatomy; dark atmosphere alone insufficient.

Pilihan artistik:

The clurichaun (clúrachán, Irish clobhair-ceann) is a mischievous fairy of Irish folklore who loves drink and haunts cellars; related to the leprechaun. Like the leprechaun, the clurichaun is a solitary fairy. In The Haunted Cellar (Thomas Crofton Croker, 1825), Naggeneen haunts an Irish lord's cellar, 6 in tall, face like a withered apple.

Onryō

Michizane as resentful spirit before later deification

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Dasar sumber dan audit

In ancient Japan, onryō were spirits of the resentful dead believed to cast curses. Onryō were feared as causes of plague and disasters and were pacified. Michizane was a scholar and statesman who died in exile. In legend disasters and plagues followed his death; he was pardoned and promoted to appease his spirit, but calamities continued. The account concerns Sugawara Michizane.

Individually viewed: solitary human-shaped court scholar Michizane with black formal court cap, draped robe, coherent normal hands and shoes, angry storm over palace roofs. Court tablet and robe details are illustrative historical context. Resentful supernatural disaster aura without invented ghost/monster deformation. No text/watermark.

Hödekin

Little felt-cap Hildesheim castle spirit

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Aura: One small human-shaped German castle house spirit Hödekin, an old sober-faced male in humble archaic clothes and a LITTLE FELT PILEUS CAP, sitting silently at the threshold of a bishop's medieval stone castle kitchen at night. Normal human face, rounded ears and human limbs, both hands visible. The tiny felt hat identifies him; eerie watchful house-spirit aura conveyed by empty kitchen, dim hearth and long shadow. No violent scene, blood, toads, horns or animal anatomy.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; dwarf-like house spirit wearing the defining little felt hat.
Dasar sumber dan audit

Hödekin (also Hödeken, Hütgin, Hüdekin and Hütchen) is a kobold (house spirit) of German folklore; the name, a diminutive meaning 'Little Hat', refers to the pileus cap he wears. Hödekin is known for haunting the castle of Bishop Bernard (Bernhardus) of the Prince-Bishopric of Hildesheim in Lower Saxony; in some versions he inhabits Winzenburg, a county he helped the bishopric obtain. Hödeken is the castle spirit of Winzenburg, a dwarf-like figure (kobold) who also carried messages between Hildesheim and Winzenburg; the road between Winzenburg and Hildesheim's Domhof that he reputedly ran to report the last count of Winzenburg's death is called the 'Rennstieg'. Per the Brothers Grimm, Hödeken was a spirit at the court of the Bishop of Hildesheim who kept the night watch awake, gave the bishop military advice, warned him of dangers, sometimes helped other townsfolk, and was once asked to guard a man's wife in his absence. Per the Grimms, he wore a little felt hat, whence the name Hütchen, in Low Saxon Hödeken.

Individually viewed: small human-shaped old house spirit sitting in stone castle kitchen threshold with defining little felt pileus cap, normal rounded ear, coherent folded hands and two boots. Quiet watchful mood without violent scene or invented monster anatomy. No text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Small human-shaped felt-cap spirit at kitchen threshold, coherent two hands/feet and rounded ears, watchful expression. No visible image text or watermark.

Alasan penolakan lama: Short human man with hat; no nonhuman anatomical features.

Knights of Ålleberg

One representative sleeping armored Ålleberg ghost knight

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; dead knight asleep in full armour under Ålleberg awaiting war.
Dasar sumber dan audit

According to Swedish legend, the Knights of Ålleberg are the ghosts of twelve knights killed at the Battle of Åsle in 1389; the legend exemplifies the king-asleep-in-the-mountain motif. The story has the ghosts trapped in Ålleberg mountain, awaiting a new war to wake them to fight for the country; the mountain is also thought to host trolls. The commonest legend tells of a farmer taking grain to market in Falköping; a man asked to buy the whole load, and the farmer followed him into a hall in the mountain where the knights slept in full armour. The farmer happened to knock a bridle so it jingled, and the riders woke asking whether it was time; the man who had bought the load assured them they could sleep on.

Individually viewed: one representative human ghost knight asleep in full armour in underground stone hall, coherent armored hands resting on sheathed sword, both armored legs/boots visible, bridle hangs nearby. Pilihan artistik:

In German folklore the Weiße Frauen (White Women) are elf-like spirits possibly from Germanic paganism, legends of light elves (Ljósálfar). They are beautiful enchanted creatures appearing at noon, brushing their hair in the sunshine or bathing in a brook. They may guard treasure or haunt castles, and entreat mortals to break their spell, always unsuccessfully. Jacob Grimm traced their whiteness and sunlight to the light elves (Ljósálfar) living in Álfheim under the god Freyr.

Dasar sumber dan audit

Fionn mac Cumhaill (Finn McCool) is a hero of Irish mythology and Scottish and Manx folklore, leader of the Fianna, seer and poet with a magic thumb, hunting with hounds Bran and Sceólang. Fionn's tales form the Fianna or Fenian Cycle, much narrated by his son, the poet Oisín. In Old Irish finn means white, bright, fair. Fionn has healing hands (heilende Hände); whoever he gives water (Wasser) recovers; he learns combat from his aunt (Tante) Los Lurgann and gets the sword (Schwert) Mac an Luinn from his mother Muirne.

Individually viewed: solitary fair-haired normal human hero in Gaelic-inspired wool tunic/cloak, seated with raised thumb-to-lips contemplative gesture and relaxed opposite hand, sword sheathed at side, normal ears and visible front boot. Nibelung (German) or Niflungr (Old Norse) is a personal or clan name of Germanic legend, tied to Nebel ('mist'), naming the Middle High German Nibelungenlied. Nibelung also names the king and people of a mythical land of dwarfs and giants in the Nibelungenlied, origin of the hoard of the Nibelungs. The nibelungos are a mythical people (pueblo mitológico) of Germanic (germanas) legend ruled by prince (príncipe) Nibelung, dark dwarfs (enanos oscuros) deep in the earth (tierra) mining gold (oro).

Eitri

Eitri/Sindri crafting short-handled Mjöllnir

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

  • No anatomical deformation claimed; Norse dwarf craftsman creating divine artifacts.
Dasar sumber dan audit

Eitri, also called Sindri, is a dwarf and Brokkr's brother in Old Norse religion. According to Skáldskaparmál, after Loki had the Sons of Ivaldi make Sif's hair, Freyr's ship Skidbladnir and Odin's spear Gungnir, he bet his head with Brokkr that Eitri could not match their craftsmanship. Eitri began working at his furnace while his brother worked the bellows, but a fly (sometimes thought to be Loki himself) stung Brokkr to stop him and spoil the items. Eitri succeeded in making the golden boar Gullinbursti, the golden ring Draupnir and the hammer Mjöllnir, winning his brother the bet, though its handle was shorter than it should have been.

Individually viewed: human-shaped bearded dwarf smith with coherent hammer and tongs hands at stone forge, creating short-handled Mjollnir-shaped hammer. Normal rounded human ear/face, no Marvel likeness or animal body, forge details illustrative. Lower body behind anvil but identifying craft anatomy clear. No readable text/watermark.

Independently viewed original: Norse human-shaped smith with coherent hammer/tongs hands crafting short-handled Mjollnir; artifact ornament is artistic treatment. No visible image text or watermark.

Slattenpatte

Danish Suså elf-woman with documented elongated chest, fully clothed

Varian makhluk yang didukung sumber.

Dasar sumber dan audit

Slattenpatten or slattenlangpat (literally 'flaccid breasts' or 'saggy long breast') is a female creature of Danish folklore. Slattenpatten or Slattenlangpat is a female being of Danish folk belief, especially tied to Suså; also called ellekvinde, ellekone or elverkone, and unlike the usually very beautiful ellepige she is marked by extraordinarily long breasts. Her breasts hang down to the waist but can be thrown over the shoulder so a child on her back can feed, and when chased she flings them over her shoulders; in southern Zealand legends tell of her being hunted by 'Kong Volmer' (really Valdemar Atterdag) or 'Wojens Jæger' with hounds. Another legend says her breasts were long so she could suckle her fish-children under water; slattenlangpat is therefore also linked with water and considered a water goddess, especially in Zealand according to some archaeologists.

Individually viewed: elderly human-shaped Danish elf-woman by river with the documented extraordinary elongated pendulous chest clearly represented as two low hanging covered contours under opaque garment, reaching below waist. Covered contour depiction is intentionally literal educational morphology rather than scarf padding.
